Before, with test mode on, the notice covered the lower half of the test mode bar (or stood in the same band just above the options window), and in `/flatui move` it slid under the toolbar. Now it stands clear of the options window's title bar, the `/flatui move` toolbar and the test mode bar, at the nearest free place just under or above them, so all of them stay readable and clickable. It follows within a quarter of a second when you drag the bar, end test mode or close `/flatui move`.
- **Reload notice**: point at it to see every change that needs a reload with its value now and at login, such as "Square icons: Off now, On at login", where before the line stopped at "and 3 more". New **Undo** button, between **Reload** and **Later**, sets them all back to what the game runs since login, so no reload is needed: the notice goes, chat says "Flat UI: put back 5 changes, no reload needed.", and Home's list of recent changes gets each one, so its **Undo** can make the change again. **Undo** waits for the end of a fight and for Setup to close. The notice now fades in when it appears, and it and its **Reload notice** box in `/flatui move` are a little wider to fit the new button.
- **Test mode** bar: the **Group** choices (**Solo** to **Raid 40**) and the **Moving** switch light up under the mouse, as the options window's controls do, and every control has a one-line tooltip, such as "Raid 20: 20 sample members in groups, as Edit Mode lays them out, while you are in no raid", "Moving: Casts fill, health drifts and cooldowns run. Off holds every sample still, so a look can be judged", "Bags: Opens or closes Flat UI's bag window, as /flatui bags" and "End: Turns test mode off. Nothing was saved."
- **Options window**: buttons that sit on a row with a tip light up under the mouse again. Before, **Set up my screen** and **Ready-made looks** on the **Profiles** page, **Modules**, **Import selected** and **Export chosen** under **Share**, the **Open** button of a search result and the buttons of the tracker editor stayed flat while you pointed at them; they still show their row's tip.
- **Profiles** page: every row says what it does when you point at it, its buttons included, and the **Switch automatically** and **Share** rows too. New **Duplicate** beside **Create** under **New profile**: it copies the profile in use, its settings and frame positions, under the name you type, or as "<name> copy" (then "copy 2" to 9) with the field empty, and puts the copy in use. **Create** still starts from the defaults and the positions a new profile starts with.
- **Import profile**: the positions of a pasted profile are fitted to your screen and UI scale, as the positions a new profile starts with are, so a frame keeps its share of the screen and none lands off a smaller one. Before, they landed where they were on the other screen. A profile made on a screen of another size shows **Fit positions to this screen** (on) under the paste field, and its warning now reads "Made for 2560 x 1440. Positions are fitted to this 1920 x 1080 screen." Turn the switch off to keep the positions as the text has them. A text from an older build, which does not name its screen, is imported as before.
- **Home**: a line of **Recent changes** or **Needs a reload** lights up under the mouse, and a click opens that setting's tab, scrolls to its row and flashes it, as a search result does. Before, the lines could not be clicked.
- **Options window**: the reset arrow of a changed row names the value it puts back, as "Reset to default: On", "Reset to default: 300 ms" or "Reset to default: Soft shade", and for a colour a swatch and its code, as "Reset to the default colour: #1E1E1E". Before, it said only "Reset to default".
- **Ready-made looks**: after a click on a look that took the place of another profile, the card has **Back to <that profile>**. One click puts your profile back in use, says "Flat UI: using Main." in chat and closes the card. It also gives new characters back what they got before if the look was given to them too. The look stays in your list of profiles.
- **Flat UI button in the game menu**: with the game's taintLog on, the Escape menu now works as the game has it, and the button simply does not show for that session. Before, its hook on the menu went around Flat UI's own guard, so the menu could break while it built its buttons.
- **Health text** on the unit frames: with any format but **As the game has it**, the health bar of a dead, released or offline unit now reads **Dead**, **Ghost** or **Offline**, centred, whatever **Show** says, as setup and test mode already drew it. Before, a dead party member, your own dead frame and a ghost on the target or party frames showed an empty bar, and an offline party member showed numbers. On the target, focus, boss, target of target and focus target frames a dead unit's bar stays empty, since the game's own Dead text (the **Dead text** row) shows there.
- New **Health text** on the **Target of target frame** page, at **As the game has it** by default, which shows nothing there since the game draws no numbers on that bar. **Percent**, **Current** and the other formats now show on the target of target and the focus target, in **Small frame text size**.
- New **Low health border** (off), under **Status colours** on **Unit frames** > **All frames: bars and pictures**: the health bar border of your own frame, your party members, their pets and your raid members takes **Low health border colour** (red by default) while the member is below **Low health below** (20% by default, 5 to 50%), also in a fight. It goes away as they heal, never shows on a dead or offline member, and follows the bars' rounded corners. Nameplates keep their own **Low health border** rows.
- **Pet target frame**: the mouse over it now shows the unit's tooltip. A right-click opens its menu (Set Focus, raid markers) when the unit is also your target or is a player. Its health shows in percent at the right end. A dead unit turns the bar grey and it reads **Dead**. The bar glides to each new value instead of jumping every 0.2 seconds. The name and the percent follow **Name font**, **Name size**, **Name outline**, **Bar text font**, **Bar text outline** and **Own bar text colour** on **All frames: text**. The druid mana bar glides too.
- **Test mode** now shows the **Pet target frame** while its row is on and the game does not show it: a Training Dummy at 65% in the hostile colour, at the frame's place, repainted as **Pet target width**, the bar texture, the border or the background change. For a druid, the **Druid mana in forms** bar also shows under the player frame in caster form, at 60%. When test mode ends it goes back to showing only in Bear and Cat Form.
- **Raid frames**: the names on the raid frames and raid-style party frames now follow **Name font**, **Name size**, **Name outline** and **Name colour** (**Class**, **Reaction** and **Custom**; **White** and **As the game has it** keep the game's white). Their status text follows **Bar text font** and **Bar text outline**, as on every other unit frame. Before, they kept the game's font and white. The setup's raid cells show the same.
- New **Sound for a debuff you can dispel** (**None** by default), under **Party and raid frames** on **Unit frames** > **All frames: icons and auras**. It plays one of the usual sounds (Spell alert, Chimes, Bell, Warhorn, Raid warning, Alarm, Whisper warning) when a party or raid member gets a debuff you can remove, also in a fight. It plays at most once every 2 seconds, so a debuff on the whole raid gives one sound. It needs **Border colour of a debuff you can dispel**.
- New **Highlight under the mouse** (on), under **Frame** on **Unit frames** > **All frames: bars and pictures**: the health bar of the unit frame under the mouse brightens a little, raid frames included, so you can see which frame a click will hit. It goes away when the mouse leaves.
- New **Quest mark on enemies you need for a quest** (on), on the **Marks and icons** tab of **Nameplates**: a small quest mark beside the nameplate of every enemy the game says belongs to a quest in your log, gone once that objective is done. **Quest mark size** sets its size, and **Quest mark position** puts it **Left** (past the raid marker and the elite or rare icon), **Right** (past the level box) or **Above** the name. Questie draws its own nameplate icons, so with Questie you may want only one of the two.
- New **Channel ticks on the player bar** (on), under **Look** on the **Cast bars** page: thin marks cross your cast bar where each tick of a channel lands, such as 5 for Arcane Missiles (3 and 4 on ranks 1 and 2), 8 for Blizzard, 3 for Mind Flay and 15 for Hellfire, with or without **Flat fill**. A pushback moves them with the bar.
- **Interrupt readiness on enemy casts**: while your interrupt is ready but the caster stands out of its range, the target, focus and nameplate cast bars now take the new **Interrupt out of range colour** (grey-blue, on the **Colours** page) instead of **Interrupt ready colour**, and turn green as you close in. The gold border on the cooldown row waits until you are in range too.
- **Cast time**: the seconds on the nameplate, target and focus cast bars now take the spell name's font, size and outline (**Text font**, **Spell name size** and **Spell name outline** on nameplates; **Text size** and **Text outline** on the target and focus bars) instead of a fixed small font, so they grow with **Nameplate size**. On a nameplate whose cast bar names the cast's target they sit just past the bar's end, no longer on top of that name.
- New **Cast time on your bars** (off), on the **Text and icon** tab of **Cast bars**: the seconds left inside the right end of your own cast bar and your pet's, in the style of the target and focus bars. The game's own time from Edit Mode is hidden while it is on, so the time never shows twice.
- New **Time left on the Overpower icon** (on), on the **Marks and icons** tab of **Nameplates**: a dark swipe drains over the Overpower icon for its 5 seconds and starts again when a new dodge renews it, so a warrior can see how long is left.
- New **Entering combat sound** and **Leaving combat sound** (None), under **Entering and leaving combat** on the **Combat text** page, with the choices of **No target sound**: +Combat and -Combat can now play a sound as each shows. Test mode stays quiet.
- New **Outgoing direction** (Up) and **Incoming direction** (Down), on the **Scroll areas** tab of **Combat text**: choose whether the numbers of each scroll area move up or down, and which way Horizontal and Static numbers stack. Before, Outgoing always climbed and Incoming always fell.
- **Action buttons** (Flat UI's): the buttons now move with their bar. Moving a bar in `/flatui move`, dragging the **Action bar block** box or a bar's tab, adding or removing a bar, or changing **Stack direction** or **Line up bars** takes the buttons along within half a second out of combat. Before, the buttons you see stayed put until a reload, an Edit Mode change or a UI scale change.
- **Action buttons** (Flat UI's): the game's **Quick Keybind Mode** works on your bars. While it is open the game's own bars show in their place, with the mode's highlights, tooltips and key capture, and a chat line says so. Flat UI's buttons come back when it closes, or as a fight starts. Before, the mode highlighted nothing on the bars and bound no key.
- **Bar block**, **Fade the block**: with Flat UI's buttons the buttons themselves now fade. Before, they never faded: the game's round button frames showed around them instead, and `/flatui status` counted that as the game showing its bars again. The block also waits **Wait before fading** (new, 500 ms) before it fades, eases down over **Fade time** (new, 300 ms) and comes back to full at once, so a mouse passing over the bars no longer makes them flicker.
- **Action bars**, **Bar background**: the panels now show behind Flat UI's buttons, and they hide and fade with them. Before, the panels were invisible with Flat UI's buttons.
- **Action buttons** (Flat UI's): Attack blinks red while you auto attack, and Auto Shot and Shoot blink while they repeat, every 0.4 seconds, as on the game's bars. Before, they did not blink.
- New **Flash when ready** row (off by default) on **Colours and cooldowns**, under **Cooldowns**: when a cooldown longer than the global cooldown ends, a quick white flash goes over the icon on Flat UI's buttons. The global cooldown never flashes.
- **Hotkey text**, **Short (M4, WU, N1, Sp)**: more long key names are now short: Spacebar, Backspace, Delete, Insert, Home, the arrow keys, Num Lock, Escape and Enter become Sp, Bs, Del, Ins, Hm, Up, Dn, Lt, Rt, NL, Esc and Ent. **Keybind on icons** of the cooldown icons follows the same setting, so Mouse Button 4 reads M4 there too instead of being cut off.
- **Proc alerts**: the large art beside the character has a **Proc art** box in `/flatui move`, so you can put it anywhere. It starts in the middle of the screen, and **Art height** still raises or lowers it from the box. Before, it was always centred and only **Art height** moved it.
- **Glows and animations** (Flat UI's buttons): **Cast fill animation** and **Interrupt flash** now work. The button of the spell you cast fills from the bottom in the cast bar colour over the cast time (it empties over a channel), fades as the cast lands and flashes red when the cast is interrupted. **Hide bar frame**, **Hide end caps**, **Hide dividers** and **Rotation helper highlight** only apply with the game's buttons, so they are now greyed while Flat UI's buttons are in use.
- **Ping bar**: while a ping waits, a click on another button of the bar now switches to that ping, and a click on the waiting one cancels it. A Shift-click with a target pings your target at once, as it does when nothing waits. The waiting button lights up in its ping's colour until the ping is placed or cancelled, and the line by the bar says so. Before, the screen-wide click that places a ping also covered the bar. A click on another button then sent the waiting ping to the world behind the bar, often the sky, which the game refused. A click between the buttons now does nothing.
- New **Icon tooltips** (On by default, Out of combat only, Off) on **Party cooldowns** and **Enemy cooldowns**. Pointing at a party icon, or at an icon of the enemy target and focus bars, names the ability with its class in its kind's colour and names the member or enemy. It says Ready or, in dim text, Ready in 1:42. A party member who sends his cooldowns reads Shared by Flat UI. Clicks still go through to the party frame, and icons on nameplates take no mouse.
- **Defensives on you** and **Your buffs**, **Icon size**: icons already showing now change size as you move the slider, out of a fight or as soon as the game opens buffs. Before, they kept their old size until you logged in again. **Your buffs** gets a **Spacing** row (3 by default), as **Defensives on you** has.
- **Healer mana**: a healer's bar turns red below the new **Low mana at** (30% by default, 0 for never). This works in a fight too, where the game hides the number. With the new **Show who is drinking** (on), a cup and Drinking show on the bar of a healer who drinks, out of combat. Test mode shows both on its sample bars.
- **Threat list**: your own bar has a thin white outline (**Mark your own bar**, on). In a new **Warning** section, **Warn near aggro** (on) makes your bar pulse red while you are not tanking and your share is at **Warn at** (90%) or above. **Warning sound** (Raid warning) plays once for that enemy. It works where the game shows threat, not on bosses, and test mode previews it on your sample bar.
- **Group tools**: during a ready check the tick shows how many are ready, as 3/5, green when all are and amber when someone is not. Its tooltip names who is not ready and who has not answered, and the result stays 8 seconds after the check ends. During a countdown the pull button counts the seconds left in amber. It goes back to its length when the countdown ends or is stopped.
- **Cooldown pulse**: the name of the spell or item now shows under the icon and fades with it (**Name under the icon**, on), so two trinkets or potions with look-alike icons can be told apart. New **Grow as it fades** (off) swells the icon by a quarter as it fades out.
- **Party cooldowns**: new **Border colour by kind** (on, the look it had). A new **Ready again** section has **Flash when ready** (off), which flashes an icon white for half a second as its cooldown ends, as on **Enemy cooldowns**.
- **Threat list** and **Healer mana**: new **Bar texture** row (Same as the Flat UI page, or any of the textures), applied at once, as **Absorb shields**, **Trackers** and **Your buffs** have.
- **Bags**, Sections: click a section's title to fold it to one line, as in "Trade goods  24  +", and click again to open it. Its right-click menu now has **Fold** or **Unfold**, and **Unfold every section** while one is folded. The same sections fold in the bag and bank windows and for all your characters, and stay folded between sessions. While you search, a folded title says how many of its items match ("3 of 24") in the Highlight colour, and an item you use from a folded section stays in it. The new row **Folded sections** on the **Bags** page opens one or all. Before, a click on a section's title did nothing, and a big section always showed every slot.
- New **Bag slots** (on) on the **Bags** page: a row over the bag window's footer with your backpack, four bags and reagent bag. It is there also while the game's bag bar is hidden (**Hidden frames** > **Bag bar**) or faded away. Drop a bag on a slot to put it in or swap it, drag or click a slot to pick its bag up, and point at one for the game's tooltip of that bag and its free slots. Not in a fight. Before, the bag window had no bag slots, so with the bag bar hidden there was no way to put in a new bag.
- **Bags**: pointing at a bag on the game's bag bar, or in the new bag slot row, dims every slot of the bag window that is not in that bag, so you can see what sits in your soul bag, quiver or a new bag. Moving away gives the window back as it was, the search's dimming included. Before, nothing happened.
- **Sell junk button**: its tooltip says what the click brings, as in "Sells 7 items for 1g 23s 40c" ("for at least" while a price has not loaded). The tooltip of the **Junk** title says what your junk is worth at a merchant, also for another character's bags and for the bank. Before, neither named an amount.
- **Bags**, the search box: the help with the search words shows only while you point at an empty box you are not typing in, and goes away as you click in or type. It no longer covers the top rows of the window, the slots the search lights. A box that holds a search shows a short tip instead ("Searching: #boe q>=rare").
- **Bags**: right-click the search box for **Quick searches**: New items, Junk, Bind on equip, Quest items, Cannot use, Consumables, Trade goods, Green and better and Epic and better, each with its term at the right. The search typed now is ticked, and **Clear** ends it. Before, the search words were only in the box's tooltip.
- New key **Flat UI bags: search** under Flat UI in Key Bindings: it opens the bag window if it is closed and puts the cursor in its search box, so you can just type. It works in a fight too. Escape leaves the box, and a second Escape closes the window as before.
- **Bags**, the gold tooltip under the bag window, and the **Gold** slot of **Data panels**: a **This session** line under the list says what the character you play made (in green) or spent (in red) since it logged in, kept over a /reload. The Gold slot keeps its width. Before, neither said how a session went.
- **Bags**, **Bag by bag**: each bag's label starts with that bag's own icon, the backpack's and keyring's included, for your bags, the bags in your bank and those of your other characters.
- **Bags**, at the banker with Flat UI's bank window: a section's right-click menu has **Put Trade goods in the bank** in the bag window and **Take Trade goods out** in the bank window, with the section's own name. Each moves the whole section one item at a time, as **Deposit** and **Take out** do, with no search typed, and says the same line in chat. Not for **Keep in place**, whose items stay in their pinned slots. Before, you had to type a search such as #trade first.
- **Tooltip position**: a tooltip that follows the cursor no longer lands on the button or unit you point at near the edge of the screen. Where it would pass the right or the top edge (the vertical action bars on the right, the buffs and minimap at the top right), it goes to the cursor's other side, mirrored, and comes back once there is room.
- **Health text on the bar**, a new row on the **Tooltips** page (**Percent** by default, or **Current / max**, **Current and percent** or **None**), writes the unit's health on the tooltip's health bar in a small outlined text, as 64% or 1.2k / 2k, kept up to date while you point at the unit, also in a fight. Before, the bar showed no number at all.
- **Show durability** on the Character window now has a tooltip: point at "Durability 62%" for each item that wears out, lowest first, in its quality's colour with its percent in white, yellow or red, then **All items** and, where the game gives it, about what a repair costs. The line takes the mouse only over its words, and clicks still reach the page.
- **Text around the map**, on the **Minimap** page: the coordinates, durability, frame rate and time lines now answer the mouse. Point at the coordinates for the zone and subzone, at the durability for each item you wear, at the frame rate for your home and world latency and Flat UI's memory, and at the time for your local and the realm's time. Shift-click the coordinates to put "Elwynn Forest 45.3, 62.1" in the chat box; click the time for the game's stopwatch and alarm, Shift-click it for the calendar. The map's own clicks are untouched.
- **Flat addon buttons**, a new row on the **Minimap** page (on), gives the other addons' buttons Flat UI gathers beside or under the map a square dark plate with a thin border in place of their round gold ring and dark disc, with a light square when you point at one. A button you tick in **Leave in place**, or the row off, gets its own art back.
- **Clicks on the bar**, a new row on the **Experience and menu bars** page (on): Shift-click the experience or reputation bar to put your progress in the chat box, ready to send, as "Level 34: 12,345 / 27,000 (45%), 1h 20m to level" or "Stormwind: Honored 4,200 / 12,000 (35%)". A click on the reputation bar opens your reputations. The tooltip ends with a dim hint.
- **Level up line in chat**, a new row on the **Experience and menu bars** page (on): each new level says in chat how long the level you finished took and your play this session, as "Flat UI: level 35, after 1h 42m at level 34 (2h 10m of play this session)." When that time is not known (the first level of a session without /played), it gives the session's play alone.
- **Experience colour** and **Rested colour**: the fill turns **Rested colour** while you are rested only while **Rested experience** is on, as the game's bar turns blue. With it off, the fill is always **Experience colour**; before, it still turned blue while that colour's row was greyed out.
- **Test mode** now shows the experience bar: while the real bar has nothing to show (at the top level, or with no faction watched), a sample bar shows at its spot, experience at 45% with a rested and a quest stretch and Stormwind at Honored under it, drawn with every row of **Experience and menu bars** and findable in `/flatui move`.
- **Data panels**: a click on **Bag space** opens or closes Flat UI's bag window while **Bags** is on, and a right click on any Flat UI slot opens the **Data panels** page of the options. Each slot's tooltip now ends with a dim line saying what its clicks do. Before, no slot but **Equipment set** took a click.
- **Data panels**: two new slots. **Time** shows the time as the game's own clock is set (local or realm, 12 or 24 hours), and its tooltip has both times and how long this session has run. **Quest log** shows "Quests 12/20", red when the log is full, and its tooltip has how many quests are ready to hand in and their experience.
- **Cursor ring**: the ring and its swipes hide while you hold the mouse to turn the camera and come back on the cursor when you let go, where before the ring stayed frozen where the cursor had been. With **In combat only** the ring fades in as a fight starts and out as it ends, instead of popping on and off.
- **Train all button**: the button says how many skills it will learn, "Train all (4)", and reads "Train all", greyed, when there is nothing to learn.
- **Make room in full bags**: the button under the loot window names the grey stack it will delete, "Delete [icon] Broken Fang x3", where before it read "Make room: delete a grey item". A long name is cut short.
- **Repair**: a merchant visit that repairs and sells greys says it in one chat line, "Sold 9 items for 38s 2c and repaired for 1g 4s.", instead of two. A repair alone, or a visit that sells nothing, keeps its own line, and so do the guild-gold lines.
- New **Mark the reward worth most** (on) on **Errands** > **Quests and levelling**: with a choice of quest rewards, a gold coin sits in the corner of the one a merchant pays most for (sell price times count), and its tooltip ends with "Sells for 1g 20s, the most of these rewards". There is no coin on a tie, when no reward sells, or while the game has not said a price. You still choose.
- New **Mana gem first in a fight** (on) on **Drink and health macros**: for a mage, **Flat UI Drink** in a fight takes your best mana gem, then your best mana potion at the next press, and starts with the gem again after the fight, as **Flat UI Health** does with the healthstone. A gem still cooling waits behind a potion that is ready.
- New **Glow colour** under **Glow on the nearest object** on **World and cursor**: **Gold** (the look so far, the default) or **By what it is**, where herbs glow green, ore copper, quest objects gold, and loot and chests white.
- **Fading**: the **Main action bar** and **Action bar 2** to **8** rows fade Flat UI's own bars while Flat UI's buttons are in use. Before, they faded the game's bars, which Flat UI keeps hidden then, so those rows did nothing.
- **Search** in the options window finds **Duplicate profile**.

## 0.5.32

- For WoW: Forever 1.60.1 build 70938. The interface number stays 16001.
- **Show thorns**: the line on the character page no longer runs under the weapon slots. It shows the total alone ("Thorns 34"), with a shield spike after it only where that fits ("Thorns 34, +20-30 on block"), sits under the right column of slots clear of the main hand, off hand, ranged and ammo slots, and is cut short with "..." rather than spill. The total of each school, which the line used to carry, is in its tooltip with each source, the spike and the total.
- New **Ping bar** page (on), under **Group**: a bar of buttons for the game's pings, with the game's own ping icons, **Ping** (the game picks the kind for what you ping), **Attack**, **Warning**, **On My Way** and **Assist**, in the order of the game's ping wheel. A click pings your target for your group, also in a fight, as the game's `/ping` does; **With no target** chooses what a click or key pings while you have no target: **The ground under the cursor** (the default), **You** or **Nothing**. Each ping has a key of its own under **Flat UI** in the game's Key Bindings (**Ping: Attack** and so on, with **Ping: Not a threat** and **Ping: Threat**, which the bar does not show), and the tooltip of each button names the ping, its key and the game's own key for it. **Show**: **In a group** (the default), **Always** or **Never, only its keys**. The bar hides while the game's ping system or **Enable Pings** is off, or while your group lets only its leader, its assistants or its tanks and healers ping and you are not one; while a fight holds it shown its buttons grey and their tooltips say why. **Cooldown swipe** (on) shows on the buttons when you can ping again after a ping from the bar. **Button size**, **Spacing**, **Layout** (**Horizontal** or **Vertical**) and **Borders in the ping's colour** (on) set its look, `/flatui move` places it (**Ping bar**, just under the group tools), and test mode shows it with a sample swipe.

## 0.5.31

- For WoW: Forever 1.60.1 build 70938. The interface number stays 16001.
- **Combat text**, **Combat text for you**: each hit, heal or word on you shows once, where some showed twice. The game's own Scrolling Combat Text for Self kept drawing its numbers beside Flat UI's after a fresh login (the game puts back the values it keeps for your account after Flat UI has turned it off), after the game's options or another addon turned it on, and once loaded it never heard Flat UI turn it off again until a `/reload`. Flat UI now turns it off again each time it comes back on and tells the game's text through the game's own setting, so it stops at once. In a raid, with **Where friendly numbers show** on, a hit on you also showed over your own raid frame or your own nameplate from the other names the game gives you; those copies are left out while **Combat text for you** shows. Two equal hits in a row still show as two.

## 0.5.30

- For WoW: Forever 1.60.1 build 70938. The interface number stays 16001.
- **Gear comparison**, **Overall** line: each spell you chose now counts as often as a minute of [WoWSims](https://github.com/wowsims/classic)' rotation for your class and spec uses it, where before every spell that is not a swing or a shot took an even share of the minute's 40 global cooldowns. The rotations are what the WoWSims Classic simulator does with each spec's own presets in a three-minute fight against a level 63 boss: with a warrior's default lines, a Fury warrior's Bloodthirst counts 8 casts a minute and Whirlwind 4, where each took a third of the 40 before, and Heroic Strike counts what it adds over the swing it replaces on 81% of your main hand's swings, where it took a third of the 40 too. With the swings it replaces, which your auto attack counts, Heroic Strike now comes out as in a fight, where it deals about as much as all your melee swings (34% of a Fury warrior's damage against 28%); its own gain over those swings is about 15%. Arms counts it on half your swings, a Protection warrior on nearly all, and a bear's Maul on 96%. Each spell keeps its own uses a minute whichever others you chose, and a spell the rotation never casts counts nothing. The spec is your druid form, else your tank spec while your role is tank, else the talent tree with the most points: Arms, Fury and Protection warriors, Retribution and Protection paladins, hunters (the Marksmanship run stands in for the other trees), Combat rogues, Shadow priests, Elemental and Enhancement shamans, Fire and Frost mages, Demonology and Destruction warlocks, Balance druids and feral druids in Cat or Bear Form. Healers, the other trees and healing lines keep the even share. The window's Overall tooltip names the rotation, as in "WoWSims' rotation for your class (Fury warrior)", and the **Overall change** tip says how it counts. Best for me in the item finder and the slot an item's tooltip shows follow the same minute.
- New **Lava tick timer** (**In lava zones** by default) on **Helpers** > **World and cursor**, under **Lava walking**: after a fire hit on you in a place with lava (Molten Core, Blackrock Depths, Blackrock Spire, Ragefire Chasm, Searing Gorge, Burning Steppes and Un'Goro Crater, the Blackrock Mountain hub with them), a bar under your character counts down to the next tick, 2 seconds on, and marks when to be in the air: its last **Jump lead** (400 ms) is a green window, and crossing into it says **Jump**, with a flash (**Flash when it is time to jump**, on) and the **Jump sound** you pick (None by default). A tick says **Hit** and starts the count again; a tick that does not come while you were in the air says **Dodged**. The countdown takes your latency off, as the server judges where it last heard you were. The bar goes 5 seconds after the last tick and shows nothing until a fire hit lands, so it stays out of the way. It is an estimate: the game tells add-ons that fire hit you, not that it was lava (the combat log is closed to them), so out of combat or while you swim any fire hit there counts, and in a fight only one that keeps the 2-second rhythm does; the first tick of each visit always lands. **Anywhere** counts fire hits in every place. **Lava bar width**, **Lava bar height**, **Lava bar colour** and **Jump colour** set its look, and `/flatui move` places it (**Lava tick timer**, just under your character). `/flatui lava` says the mode, the place with its instance and map IDs, the ticks seen, dodged and not seen, the last hit on you, the last gaps between ticks and the latency used; `/flatui lava test` shows a sample for 8 seconds, and `/flatui lava trace` prints a line for each hit on you with what the timer made of it. Test mode shows the sample too. The idea is the timing bar of the Classic WeakAura "Avoid lava damage"; none of its code is used.
- New **Show thorns** (on) in the **Character window** section of **Item slots**, under **Bags and items**: the damage an attacker takes each time it strikes you in melee, on the character page under the right column of slots, as one total a hit by school ("Thorns 45 (Nature 30, Arcane 15)"), grey at 0. It counts the Thorns, Retribution Aura and Fire Shield on you and other buffs with a time whose text deals damage to attackers, and the "When struck in combat inflicts" lines of your worn items, their enchantments and the set bonuses you hold; Iron, Mithril and Thorium Shield Spikes follow the total as "block 8-12" and so on, since they strike only on a block. Point at the line for each item, enchantment and buff with its school and amount. Buffs are read out of a fight; in one the line keeps those read before it, and the tooltip says so. The game's stats list is not touched.

## 0.5.29

- For WoW: Forever 1.60.1 build 70938. The interface number stays 16001.
- **Defaults** follow the author's current **Default** profile, so what you never set changes. The **Bar block** page is off by default again, so the game lays out its action bars and Flat UI's experience bar keeps its own place and **Width**; turned on, block 1 holds, from the top, action bar 3, action bar 4, action bar 2 and the main bar (its list is the main bar and action bars 2, 4 and 3, stacked upward from the first bar). **Background opacity** is 75% by default, where it was 85%. A new install's **Default** profile and every new profile start with the author's current positions, fitted to your screen as before: the tracked buff icons, the absorb shields, the action bar block, the cooldown icons, the loot rolls, the party and raid frames, the experience bar and the main hand swing timer; the other frames start at the game's or Flat UI's own place. Picking **Jon Default** among the ready-made looks places the same frames. A profile that saved the Bar block page on or off, its own bars for block 1 or its own opacity keeps them, and a profile you have keeps its positions.
- **Bags**: only the backpack and normal bags count as free space. Bags that take only certain items, a trade bag (herbs, enchanting, engineering, mining, gems, leather), a quiver, an ammo pouch, a soul bag or the reagent bag, and the keyring, which takes only keys, are no longer counted. Before, the free count under the bag window and its tooltip listed them, and in the Sections view the **Free slots** section added their empty slots to its number and, with **Every free slot**, showed each one (the keyring's too), so your bags looked roomier than they were. Now the free count under the window shows the backpack and normal bags only, with no special bags after it; its tooltip names each special bag's free slots apart, marked as not counted. In **Free slots** the title counts normal bags only and **Every free slot** shows only theirs; **One per kind of bag** still shows, in your own bags, a slot for each kind of special bag with room, with its own number, so you can drop an item into it, but that number is not in the title (the keyring gets no slot). Each special bag's label in **Bag by bag**, the keyring's too, still says how many of its slots are free. The **Bag space** data panel counts the same way: it no longer counts the reagent bag as room, its tooltip says it counts only bags that take any item, and it lists the special bags apart as before. **Buy in bulk** counts the reagent bag as room only for a crafting reagent.
- **Experience bar**: **Width** on **Experience and menu bars** now follows what the bar does. With the **Bar block** page off, as it is by default, with **Experience bar under the bar block** off, or with block 1 empty, the row is live and shows its value, and the bar takes that width; it reads "as wide as the block" and is grey only while block 1 really holds the bar under its bars. Before, it was grey and read "as wide as the block" whenever **Experience bar under the bar block** was on, although the bar used its own width while no block held it. The tip of **Experience bar under the bar block** says it works while the Bar block page is on.

## 0.5.28

- For WoW: Forever 1.60.1 build 70938. The interface number stays 16001.
- **Bar block**: block 1 now holds, from the top, action bar 3, action bar 2, the main bar and action bar 4 (its list is action bar 4, the main bar, action bar 2 and action bar 3, stacked upward from the first bar), where it held the main bar and action bars 2 to 4 before. **One size and spacing for every bar** is on by default, so action bar 4 lies flat in the block like the others once you reload, with every bar of the blocks at 12 buttons in a row of the game's size. A profile that saved its own bars for block 1, or saved the row off, keeps them. A block with no place saved starts where the main bar is, on the main bar's bottom, so the bar under it stays on the screen. The setup drawing of the page shows the same.
- **Bar block** in `/flatui move`: each bar of a block has a tab at the left end of its row, inside the block's box. Drag the tab up or down and let go, and the bar takes that place in its block; drop it on another block's box and it moves into that block. A line across the block shows where it goes while you drag, and the block lays out again as you let go. Escape, or letting go outside every box, leaves the bars as they were; nothing is dragged in combat. The **Bars** and **Add a bar** rows of the page change the same order and show what the tabs did.
- **Experience bar**: Flat UI's experience bar now sits right under the bar block by default, the block's gap between bars below block 1 and exactly as wide as the block, and it follows the block when you add or take out a bar, change the button size or the stack direction, or move the block's box in `/flatui move`. The block keeps the bar's room under its bars, so its box, its **Background panel** and its fade take the bar in, and the stance and pet bars stay above the block. New row **Experience bar under the bar block** (on) on **Experience and menu bars**: while it is on, **Width** reads "as wide as the block" and the bar's box in `/flatui move` is grey and says it follows the bar block; off, or with the **Bar block** page off, the bar has its own place and width again, the one you saved before included. A bar with nothing to show, at the top level with no faction watched, keeps no room. Jon Default, and the positions a new profile starts with, no longer place the experience bar. The setup drawing of the Bar block page shows the bar under the block.
- **Hit table**, new row **Only while Ctrl is held** (on), under **On enemy tooltips**: the hit table's lines show on an enemy's tooltip only with Ctrl. Hold Ctrl as the tooltip shows, or press it while the tooltip shows: pressing Ctrl adds the lines; they leave when the tooltip next changes, not as you let go. The tooltip is never built again for them, so its place, owner and fade stay the game's, and every tooltip after it is as the game has it; over a unit frame, whose tooltip the game sets again every 0.2 seconds, they come and go with the key. New row **Hint while Ctrl is up** (off) puts one dim line, "Hold Ctrl for the hit table", in their place while Ctrl is up, which becomes their header as you press Ctrl. Turn the row off for the lines on every enemy's tooltip, as before.

## 0.5.27

- For WoW: Forever 1.60.1 build 70938. The interface number stays 16001.
- **Jon Default**, the author's own profile, is the baseline for everyone: its 105 settings are now the defaults, so every setting you never changed takes Jon's value, and the **Standard** look, every setting at its default, is the same. On by default now: **Errands** (invites from friends and your guild, and resurrections), **Data panels** (its two panels still off until you show them), **Threat list**, **Healer mana**, **Drink and health macros**, **Group tools**, **Cooldown pulse** (for cooldowns of 5 seconds and more), **Fading** (also the micro menu and the bag bar, to 90% after 0.1 seconds, and in combat too), **Place the tooltip** (every tooltip, 69 across and 48 down), **Hide error messages**, **Entering and leaving combat**, **Custom UI scale** with **Scale below 65%** at 60%, **Borderless**, the **Soft shade** bar texture, the cursor ring, once you turn it on, at 32 in lilac with the global cooldown and a blue cast, and many rows of the nameplates, combat text, action bars, cast bars, cooldown icons, trackers, enemy cooldowns, unit frames, experience bar, chat and windows (the options window shows each default). Your profile keeps every setting you saved.
- A new install's **Default** profile and every new profile now start with Jon Default's frame positions (27 frames: the player, target, party and raid frames, the loot window, the bags and the bank, the cooldown icons and viewers, the trackers, the swing timers, the experience bar and the rest), fitted to your screen: each offset is scaled by the size of your screen, in interface units at the scale the profile gives, against Jon's (2560 by 1440 pixels at 60%), so a frame keeps its share of the screen and none lands off a smaller one. Picking **Jon Default** among the ready-made looks places them the same way. A profile you have keeps its positions, a copy of a profile takes that profile's, an imported text its own, and **Game look** none, with **Custom UI scale** off. The other ready-made looks no longer list what is now the default.
- **Profiles**: a character you had given a profile could log in on **Default** instead, and stay there until you picked its profile again. Flat UI found a character's profile by its name and realm alone, and the game does not always say them yet when Flat UI loads. Now each character also keeps the name of its profile in its own saved settings, which need no name, and Flat UI reads the name and realm again at login and puts the character's profile in use before anything starts. A saved character is never taken for a new one, so **Profile on a new character** no longer puts another profile over its own. A profile renamed or deleted on another character is followed as before.
- **Fading**, **Micro menu**: the row did nothing while **Leave the game's action bars alone** was on, as it is by default. It now fades the micro menu whatever that row says: its buttons and the help ticket button fade together, and the mouse over any of them, the help ticket button above or under the menu included, shows them all. New rows **Micro menu faded opacity** and **Bag bar faded opacity** (**As Faded opacity**, or 0%, 10%, 25% or 50%) give the micro menu or the bag bar a faded opacity of its own in place of the page's **Faded opacity**, the other rows keeping theirs. At 0% it is gone until the mouse comes over it, and its buttons still take clicks where they are. Where the game lends the micro menu to a vehicle's bar it shows there as the game has it.
- **Hidden frames**, **Micro menu**: the row hid nothing while **Leave the game's action bars alone** was on. It now hides the micro menu whatever that row says, the way **Bag bar** hides the bag bar: its buttons and the help ticket button go with it, and C, P, L and the other keys and Escape still open their windows. Only the menu goes and its frame stays, empty and the same size, so the main bar, which the game hangs from that frame, and the cast bar, swing timers and extra action button, which the game stacks by it, keep their places. Out of a vehicle in a fight the menu shows until the fight ends. `/flatui move` still does not move or size the micro menu while that row is on: the game hangs its main bar from it and measures its bottom bars by it, so placing it would place the main bar, which that row keeps Flat UI away from.
- **Bar block** is on by default and holds the main bar and action bars 2 to 4, also while **Leave the game's action bars alone** is on, which now leaves only the bars in no block to the game, so the action bars move with `/flatui move` like the other frames. A profile that saved the page on or off keeps its choice. The six fixed bar rows are now a list for each block: **Bars** moves a bar up or down or takes it out, and **Add a bar** adds the main bar or one of bars 2 to 8, a bar from another block leaving that one; the bars you had in the six rows move into block 1, once. Up to three blocks, each with its own bars, **Stack direction**, **Line up bars**, **Own gap between bars** and **Gap between bars**, and its own box in `/flatui move` (**Action bar block**, **Action bar block 2** and **Action bar block 3**); blocks 2 and 3 are off until you give them a bar, and stand beside block 1, on its left and its right, until you move them. At login and when you add a bar, out of combat, a bar of a block that the game's options have off is turned on there with the game's own setting for it, and chat says so; a bar in no block is left as it is, and nothing is turned off. **One size and spacing for every bar** sizes the bars of every block. **Stance, pet and experience bars** go above block 1, and stay with the game while **Leave the game's action bars alone** is on. The **Bar blocks** page still hangs its bars from block 1.
- **Bags**: drag a section's title in the bag or bank window to move the section, or right-click the title to move it to the top, up, down or to the bottom. A bar in the **Highlight colour** shows where it goes. The order is one for both windows and all your characters, New items stays at the top and Free slots at the bottom, and **Section order** puts Flat UI's order back. Where an item is filed does not change.
- **Bags**: Ctrl+right-click an item in your bags to pin its slot to it, and again to unpin it; Ctrl+left-click stays the dressing room. If Ctrl is your Auto Loot Key, the game opens an item that holds loot on that click, so drop such an item on **Keep in place** to pin it. A pinned slot has a small square at its top right: solid while its item is there, hollow while something else is, and an empty one shows the item faded. The items in their pinned slots make a new section, **Keep in place**, first by default, whose items go in the order of your bags and are never sorted; its title stays while you have a pin, also when no pinned slot holds its item. Drop an item on its title to pin the slot it came from, or a pinned item on another section to unpin it. A pinned item is taken off the sell list, and a grey one put on the keep list, so Sell junk never sells it; unpinning leaves both lists as they are. With the new row **Put pinned items back** (on), Flat UI puts each pinned item back in its slot after a clean up of your bags, while the sort button stays busy, and when that item comes back to your bags from loot, a merchant, the mail or the bank, out of combat only; a move you make inside your bags is left alone until then. A bag set to ignore clean up is never given another item, and nothing moves while the game's split box is open. The game's clean up cannot leave single items alone, so pinned items leave their slots and come back a moment later. Right-click **Keep in place** to put them back now or unpin them all, and **Pinned slots** lists them; the window is laid out again after a put back. Pins are per character, at most 100, in your bags only.
- **Flat UI setup**: **Back** now shows on every screen. Where there is a screen before, it goes there as before; on the first stop, and on **Your screen** when the walk has no stops, it leaves setup to where you opened it from: the welcome screen when you started setup there (in a fight it opens after combat), else the options window at the page it showed, Home or the page you were on. Your choices are kept as when you close setup, and `/flatui setup` continues them. Escape still closes the window.
- **Options window**: the sidebar has eleven groups, named for what their pages set: **Look**, **Frames and bars**, **Action bars**, **Combat** (your own fight: Cooldowns, Trackers, Defensives on you, Absorb shields, Enemy cooldowns, Combat text), **Group** (Party cooldowns, Threat list, Healer mana, Group tools), **Character and gear**, **HUD**, **Chat and windows**, **Bags and items**, **Helpers** (Errands, Drink and health macros, Mount key, World and cursor) and **Layout**. Every setting keeps its value and its saved path.
- **Gear comparison** is a page of its own under **Character and gear**, with **Item tooltips**, **Against**, **Tanking** and **Spells counted**, and a **Window and finder** tab for the window, its Compare gear button and the item finder; it was the second half of the **Spells and gear** tab of **Chat and tooltips**. That tab's spell lines are the **Spell tooltips** page beside it (**Lines** and **Showing**), and **Hit table** moved there from **Minimap and buffs**.
- **Minimap and buffs** is now **Minimap and HUD**, with one switch, **Restyle the minimap and HUD**, and five pages under **HUD**, beside **Data panels**: **Minimap** (the Minimap tab's rows, with Border and background), **Buffs and debuffs**, **Quests and loot** (objective tracker, loot and alerts), **Messages and timers** (error messages, zone titles and the talking head, timers, **Damage meter**, **Queue and zone bars**) and **Experience and menu bars** (bag bar and micro menu in one section). Each shows the module's switch and its Off chip, and Home has one tile for the module. **Hide the zone name** and **Hide the subzone name** are **Hide the zone title** and **Hide the subzone title**, apart from the minimap's **Zone name**.
- **Chat and tooltips** is the **Chat** page (with its **Chat text** tab) and a **Tooltips** page under **Chat and windows**; Tooltips has a **Combat and extra lines** tab with Fade, Combat, Extra lines and Item comparison. The search finds a page by its module's name too.
- **Item slots** is a page under **Bags and items** beside **Bags**, with **Clean up bags** moved in from **Windows**; its first row is **Flat item slots** and its bag section **The game's bag windows**. On **Windows**, Character, Talents and Legacy and Looking For Group are one section, **Window art**.
- **Absorb shields** is a page under **Combat**, no longer a tab of **Defensives on you**, with its switch in the header; profile sharing lists it as a module of its own.
- **Combat text** has tabs: **Scroll areas** (with Classic), **Hits and colours**, **Friendly units** and **You and warnings**. **Trackers** has a **Proc alerts** tab, **Amounts** and **Pet** are on **Low and missing**, **Tracker look** is **Look and position** and **Tracker** is **Edit a tracker**. **Errands** has a **Popups and invites** tab (Death and popups, Other players), and **Levelling** is **Quests and levelling**.
- **Flat UI** has an **Options and setup** tab (Options window, Setup, Game menu), and its World and Cursor sections are the **World and cursor** page under **Helpers**, with **Mount key** and **Drink and health macros**. On **Nameplates**, **Game settings** is **Size and clicks** and holds **Clickable area**, and **Target and marks** is **Marks and icons**. **Cooldowns**' **More rows** is **Rows 2 to 4**, **Bar blocks** is **Class and item bars**, **Tooltip** is **Tooltip position**, and Flat UI setup names its areas the same way.
- **Reload notice**: a change that needs a reload now says so on screen, wherever you make it: in the options window, in setup, with a profile switch, copy, reset or import, or with a slash command. A small notice of Flat UI's own at the top of the screen, over the options window too (just above or under it where it would cover its title bar), says what needs it, such as "3 changes need a reload: Leave the game's action bars alone, Action buttons, Square icons.", with **Reload** and **Later**. **Reload** works where the game blocks reloads for add-ons, through the game's own /reload; there it waits for the end of a fight, dimmed meanwhile, and you can still type /reload. **Later** hides the notice until your next change that needs a reload, and it goes by itself when nothing needs one any more, as when you put a change back. Move it with the **Reload notice** box in `/flatui move`. Home's **Needs a reload** now also lists the changes made before you opened the window.

## 0.5.26

- For WoW: Forever 1.60.1 build 70938. The interface number stays 16001.
- **Hit table**, **Text size** is now a dropdown of sizes, 70%, 80%, 90%, 100%, 110%, 125%, 150%, 175% and 200%, in place of the slider; a size you saved with the slider reads as the nearest of them (a tie as the larger). A pick resizes the table at once and is kept with your profile. New row **Text size on enemy tooltips** (100%, the same sizes, shown while **On enemy tooltips** is on) sizes the hit table's lines on an enemy's tooltip alone: the tooltip's other lines, and every tooltip after it, keep the game's size.
- **Hit table**: the Spell line has a new column, **Resist** (R on enemy tooltips, as in "Spells  M4 C12.3 H96 R-2.5"): the share of a spell's damage the enemy resists on average, with your Spell Piercing, which the game withholds where it withholds your hit and crit (? then). The enemy's own resistances are not known and count as none, so with piercing Resist goes below 0, in green, for the damage a creature takes more: WoW: Forever's Spell Vulnerability, where each point of piercing past 0 adds half of what a point of resistance takes (0.75 / (5 x your level)), -2.5% with 20 piercing at level 60. Against a player piercing does nothing below 0. A creature above you resists about 2% more a level (6% for a boss at 60), Classic's rule and an estimate on WoW: Forever, which piercing does not lower; a spell that also slows or roots takes no such share, since it pays for the levels with its higher miss. It takes nothing from Miss, Crit or Hit. `/flatui status` says your piercing and what Resist counts, and a dim note on an enemy's tooltip says it while R is not 0.
- **Hidden frames**, a new page under **Layout** (on, with nothing ticked): tick a frame of the game to take it off the screen, untick it to have it back as it was, where it was, shown as the game would show it. The ticks come in groups, **Action bars**, **Unit frames**, **Minimap**, **Buffs and debuffs**, **Bars and timers**, **Messages and alerts** and **Trackers and meters**: the game's HUD frames that `/flatui move` moves (the action bars, the micro menu, the bag bar, the player, target, focus, pet, party, raid and boss frames, the minimap, your buffs and debuffs, the cast bar, the objective tracker, the damage meter, the talking head, the alerts, the error text and the rest) and the parts of the minimap one by one (the zone name and the tracking, mail, calendar, clock, addon and zoom buttons); not the chat, the loot window, the tooltip, the windows or Flat UI's own frames. **Alerts** takes each toast away as it comes, and one that comes in a fight once the fight ends. The pet frame, the totems and the class resource bars go with the **Player frame** while Edit Mode keeps them under it, and their boxes in `/flatui move` say hidden with Player frame. Each tick's tip says what goes with the frame and what still works: the keys of a hidden action bar, B and the bag keys without the bag bar, the menu's keys and Escape without the micro menu. A hidden frame stays hidden when the game shows it again, in Edit Mode, at a zone change, with a new target, after a reload. A tick in a fight hides or shows its frame once the fight ends, and chat says so. In `/flatui move`, Shift and right-click a box to hide its frame (the box then says hidden) or show it again; on a box with no tick it resets nothing and says so in chat, or press **Hide frame** in the inspector; the frame list has a group **Hidden** at the top, whose eye shows a frame again. `/flatui hide` opens the page. While **Leave the game's action bars alone** is on (the default), the ticks of the game's bars, the micro menu and the game's status bars hide nothing; the **Bag bar** tick hides the bag bar either way. A frame you hide is never faded by **Fading**: it comes back at its own opacity, and fades again from there.
- **Gear comparison**: Spell Piercing on an item counts. Every damage spell of a school of magic rolled on the Spell line does the damage your piercing adds (Resist below 0, at your own level), so an item with piercing raises your spells' rows and **Overall**; piercing of one school (Fire, Frost, Nature, Shadow or Arcane Piercing), which the game does not give, is counted from the items you wear and with the item, for that school's spells alone. The share an enemy above you resists for its levels is left out, as a spell that also slows or roots pays for those with its higher miss instead. Piercing is named as not counted only when no spell compared takes it, as for a hunter's shots. The dim line under a spell's tooltip counts piercing too, and says "with crits, misses and Spell Piercing"; the window's foot says so.
- **Fading**, **Bag bar**: the row did nothing while **Leave the game's action bars alone** was on, as it is by default, so the backpack, the bag slots, the reagent bag and the keyring never faded. It now fades them all together whatever that row says, wherever the bar is moved, and the mouse over any one of them, the keyring and the reagent bag included, shows them all. Flat UI still does not move the bag bar while that row is on. A bag bar you hide on the **Hidden frames** page stays hidden, and fades again once you show it.
- **Fading**: new row **Bag windows** (off) fades your open bag and bank windows: Flat UI's bag and bank windows with **Bags** on, the game's bag windows, the keyring's and the reagent bag's among them, and its bank with it off. A window shows in full as it opens and fades after **Wait before fading**, and never fades while you use it: under the mouse, with an item on the cursor, while a stack is being split or while you type in its search box. **In combat** and **With a target** keep it shown as they do the other rows, and a window you close comes back at full opacity the next time it opens.
- **Minimap and buffs**, **Minimap**, **Zone name**, new rows **Close the gap under the zone name** (off) and **Gap under the zone name** (0 px, shown while it is on). The game leaves about 20 between the zone name and the top of the map, room for its ring, which **Hide minimap ring and header** and the **Square** shape take away, so the zone name stood apart from the map, round or square, with nothing between. On, the zone name, with the clock, calendar and tracking button beside it, comes down to the map, which stays where it is, with its border, the addon button column and the box of Edit Mode and `/flatui move`. The mail icon and the addon list button, which the game hangs between the zone name and the map, go to the other side of the zone name where they do not fit there, so they never cover the map. 0 lays the zone name on the map's border, and no gap takes it further from the map than the game's place; a line of **Text around the map** placed above the map keeps its room under the zone name while it fits there, and goes below the map otherwise. With Edit Mode's **Header underneath** the zone name comes up to the map from below. While the game's ring shows (**Hide minimap ring and header** off on a round map, or the ring of **Minimap in cities**) and while Edit Mode is open, the zone name keeps the game's place.
- **Absorb shields** is a new tab of **Defensives on you**, on by default, after the NanShield display: under your character (placed with `/flatui move`), a bar with the total of every absorb on you, and a bar for each shield, **Power Word: Shield**, **Contingency Plan**, **Ice Barrier**, **Mana Shield**, **Fire Ward**, **Frost Ward**, **Shadow Ward**, a voidwalker's **Sacrifice**, the protection potions and items that absorb, with its icon, the amount left (**Amount**: also its size, or none), the time left and its name, in the colour of the school it takes (**Colour by school**; **Shield colour** for one that takes every school). The game hides each shield's amount in a fight, so while it hides your buffs the total, which it hands to the bar unread, is the one live amount and each shield shows its time with a bar that runs down by it. **Show** (the total and each shield, the total only, each shield only), **Total bar is full at** (**Your shields** or **Your health**), **Bar width**, **Bar height**, **Spacing**, **Bars grow**, **Bar texture**, **Time strip**, a row for each class's shields, the potions and items, **Add a spell** and **Remove a spell** (`/flatui absorbs add`), a **Sound** when a shield breaks while your buffs show, and **Your target's total**, a bar of its own. `/flatui absorbs test` shows sample bars and `/flatui absorbs check` what the game gives of each shield on you.
- **Test mode**, new: shows your frames on screen with sample content while you change options, so every setting can be seen as you change it, each at its saved place, scale and size and drawn with Flat UI's look. Party frames show four members (Mara, a leader tank with a marker and aggro; Eli, a healer out of range with a debuff you can dispel and your heals over time, whose health drifts; Ivo, a warlock with a Voidwalker; Fay, a dead rogue), as cells with you first under Edit Mode's Raid-Style Party Frames. Raid frames show 10, 20 or 40 in groups at the cell size and group layout Edit Mode gives them, with every class and role, dead, offline and out of range members, debuffs you can dispel, markers on the tanks, a leader and two assistants, buffs and heals over time, as the game's raid frame options say. The target, target of target, focus, focus target, pet, boss 1 to 5 and a shaman's totem frames, cast bars that fill, channel and are interrupted, the swing timers, debuffs and buffs, two loot rolls and a sample nameplate with its cast and threat colours show too, and so do Flat UI's own frames: combat text, combat alerts, the cooldown pulse, cooldown icons, Defensives on you, Your buffs, Absorb shields, party and enemy cooldowns, trackers stepping fine, low and missing, your class's first proc alert, the threat list, healer mana, the hit table, data panels, group tools and the cursor ring. A sample steps aside as soon as the real frame shows, a frame ticked on **Hidden frames**, or one that hangs from it, gets none, and one the game has never placed is named on the bar instead. Turn it on with the **Test mode** switch on the title bar of the options window, **Samples** on the `/flatui move` toolbar, `/flatui test` (or `test on`, `test off`) or a key (Key Bindings > Flat UI). Its bar at the top of the screen (clear of the options window's title bar, and draggable) picks the group (**Solo**, **Party**, **Raid 10**, **Raid 20**, **Raid 40**), holds the samples still with **Moving**, opens Flat UI's **Bags**, **Bank**, **Gear** and **Finder**, and **End** turns it off. It ends when you close the options or `/flatui move` it was turned on from, when Setup takes the window and at a reload, pauses in a fight and saves nothing. Flat UI never opens Edit Mode or a game window and never shows the game's own unit frames: the samples are Flat UI's own frames drawn where the game's are.
- `/flatui test` used to show three sample icons for 8 seconds on Defensives on you, Your buffs, Party cooldowns and Enemy cooldowns; that is now `/flatui test icons`, and the help says so.
- **Party cooldowns**: on your own row, the end of a sample icon's sweep in `/flatui test icons` could drop the real cooldown that icon had shown before. It no longer does.

## 0.5.25

- For WoW: Forever 1.60.1 build 70938. The interface number stays 16001.
- **Gear comparison**: a weapon of a kind you have yet to train up compared badly in every row, as Quel'Serrar against a Flurry Axe did: Sunder Armor, Revenge and Shield Slam fell 9 to 11% though none of them uses the weapon, and the sword's swing barely beat the axe's. The comparison counted your low Swords skill against your Axes skill, so more strikes missed and half the swings glanced. Now every weapon counts at the full skill of its line for your level, with what your race and gear add, now and with the item alike, so the rows show the items and not your training: such a strike changes only through the hit, crit and stats the item gives, and the weapon skill your race or gear gives its kind of weapon. A note names a compared weapon you have yet to raise, as in "Swords 230/275: until you raise it you miss more", in the window and in the item's lines. The hit table under the player frame and on enemy tooltips, and the line under a spell's tooltip of what a cast does with crits and misses, still count your skill as it is; pointed at in the window, a weapon spell's row says so.
- **Gear comparison**, the **Overall** line and **Best** and **Worst**: Attack's row said -1.1% while **Worst** said Attack -25.8%, and **Overall** was the plain mean of the rows' percents, a swing's counted as much as a Shield Slam's. Attack counted one swing at **A cast**, which set a 2.00 weapon's swing against a 1.50 weapon's, but a second in the threat behind **Overall** and **Worst**. Now your auto attack, Auto Shot, wand and seals count a second everywhere ("a second" on the row, and "a second" in place of "dps" in the item's lines), and **Overall** is what your spells do in a minute with the item against now: your swings for the whole minute, the other spells of each kind an even share of the minute's global cooldowns, Heroic Strike, Cleave, Maul and Raptor Strike only what they add over the swing they take the place of, each spell times its weight, so the weights count against each other; damage and healing as at **A cast** whatever the switch shows, threat as at **Threat**, for a tank or in the window while the switch says Threat; pointed at, it says so. **Best** and **Worst** come from the rows on show, a gain and a loss, as their rows say them (none under **Avoidance**), and the slot an item shows in its lines, and the one the window's notes put first, go by the same minute. The notes read better too: a note cut short ends at a word with "...", where it left "300 Armor,;"; a chance on hit is named after its item, as in "Not counted: Quel'Serrar's chance on hit (Grants the wielder...)", and so is that of the weapon it replaces or the item of **Against**; the window notes every slot it shows, and an item's lines only the slot they show; Stamina and Spirit say they are not counted, once; "Not wearable yet" says the level the numbers are for; ~ marks Now and With each for its own side and no longer stands before a 0% on the **Overall** line; and the foot no longer reads as if enchants were not counted.

## 0.5.24

- For WoW: Forever 1.60.1 build 70938. The interface number stays 16001.
- **Combat text**, **Whose hits** at **Yours**: more of your hits show with the right spell, among them ticks every 2 seconds, critical ticks, your pet's hits, **Retaliation** and a strike cast while a damage over time ticks. When a swing and a strike land together, a weapon strike such as **Mortal Strike** takes the larger hit and a fixed one such as **Hamstring** the smaller, and where the game withholds the amounts, as this build does, both show with no spell name or icon rather than a wrong one.
- **Combat text**, scroll areas: in a fight the numbers of **Arc** (and **Straight**) now always start level with the handle of their area, on both sides, and follow the path of their style. Until now a number that came before the one in front of it had moved a line started a line further back, lower in **Outgoing** and higher in **Incoming**, and kept that place all the way. In a burst each number started further back than the one before, so the numbers sank down the screen. Now a number that comes while the one before is still near the start takes the next free lane beside it: the same path moved sideways by about the width of a number, first away from your character and then towards it, four lanes at most. A burst, such as **Whirlwind** on several enemies, **Sweeping Strikes** or a critical hit with its procs, shows side by side, four numbers at once. Only when all four lanes are still busy at the start does a number wait for the lane that frees first, 0.3 seconds at most after it comes or after the number ahead of it in that lane, and never more than 1.2 seconds, so every number of a burst of up to 20 shows clear of the others and a long flood of hits, such as **Blizzard** on many enemies, never falls more than 1.2 seconds behind. A lane is three times as wide as a number is high, so it grows with **Size** and **Size of your text**. Each area now holds 32 numbers at once, where it held 16 (**Outgoing**) and 12 (**Incoming**), so a large burst no longer takes its first numbers off before they are seen. `/flatui status` counts the numbers that waited for a lane, and the numbers taken off early because all 32 of an area's numbers were still on screen. **Horizontal** and **Static**: when every row is busy, the oldest number goes and the new one takes its row, where before the two lay on top of each other. Each number is centred again after its font is set, so its pop grows from its middle. The setup drawing of combat text shows the lanes.

## 0.5.23

- For WoW: Forever 1.60.1 build 70938. The interface number stays 16001.
- **Gear comparison**: the **Compare gear** button on the Character window could not be clicked. The game raises the Character window above everything in its layer each time it is clicked, which put the button under the window's background and gave its clicks to the window's title bar. The button is now one layer above the window, so it stays on top and clickable.
- **Gear comparison**: the **item finder**'s search box could not be clicked. Where the screen has no room for the finder right of the **Gear comparison** window, as at the game's own UI scale on a 16:9 screen, the finder opens on the window's left, over the Character window. The game puts the Character window in front of everything in its layer each time you click it and each time one of its windows opens or closes, so the Character window lay over the search box and took its clicks. The finder now lies one layer above the window, where the Character window and the game's other windows no longer cover it, and its search box and level boxes stay above the rest of the finder and above the **Compare gear** button, also on the title bar of a minimized finder, where the search box meets that button on the Character window's title bar. The **Gear comparison** window now comes to the front when it opens or is asked for again, so a game window it opened under no longer takes the clicks and drops meant for its item, **Against** and weights.

## 0.5.22

- For WoW: Forever 1.60.1 build 70938. The interface number stays 16001.
- **Gear comparison**: the button on the Character window (**Button on the Character window**, **Spells and gear** tab of **Chat and tooltips**) is now a labelled **Compare gear** button, Flat UI's gear icon and the words, 20 high in the look of the **Windows** page, on the Character window's title bar right of the title and left of the close button, clear of both and of the stats pane button. Its words light up while the **Gear comparison** window is open. It takes the size of the Character window when you size that window, so it stays clear of the close button at any size. Where the title would run into it, as a long name in the narrow Character window can, the words fold away and the gear icon stays. It now comes and goes with the Character window however the window opens, also when it was open as you logged in or reloaded: Flat UI looks a few times a second while the row and **Chat and tooltips** are on, and at once when the game opens or closes the window. New box **Compare gear button** in `/flatui move`, shown while the Character window is open: drag it anywhere and the button keeps that place on the Character window, moving with it, as your profile keeps every moved frame; a right-click on the box puts it back on the title bar. Clicks, the drop of an item and the tooltip are as before.
- **Minimap and buffs**, **Tracker, loot and alerts**, **Loot rolls** (on): a roll now has Flat UI's look all through: a flat background and thin border in place of the game's toast, the item's icon cropped (**Icon crop**) with a thin border in its quality colour, the name in that colour, a flat timer bar in your **Bar texture** with a thin border, across the foot of the roll, and **Need**, **Greed** (or **Transmog**) and **Pass** in a row at the right. Off gives the rolls the game's look back. The rolls also move: drag the new **Loot rolls** box in `/flatui move`, sized for four rolls and starting where the game stacks them. Until you move it the game places the rolls as before; once you have, they stack from there with the game's spacing, also in a fight, and new row **Rolls stack** (**Up**, as the game stacks them, or **Down**) says which way. A right-click on the box gives them back to the game.
- **Nameplates**, **Target and marks**, new row **Overpower on enemy nameplates** (on, warriors only): when an enemy dodges you, Overpower's icon shows above its name, centred, with Flat UI's thin border, at **Overpower icon size** (26). It glows with the calm proc glow of the action buttons while you can use Overpower now, and is grey, dim and still while you cannot: the wrong stance, too little rage, or its cooldown (the global cooldown does not count). It goes when you cast Overpower, when its 5 second window ends, or when the enemy dies or its nameplate goes, and a later dodge of yours moves it. The game does not say whose attack was dodged and the combat log is closed to addons, so a dodge counts as yours when that enemy is your target and you are in melee with it, or when Overpower turns usable within half a second of it (your target first, else the latest dodge). The icon is made on each enemy nameplate out of combat, so a nameplate first seen in a fight gets its icon when the fight ends; `/flatui status` counts those.

## 0.5.21

- For WoW: Forever 1.60.1 build 70938. The interface number stays 16001.
- **Gear comparison** window: the **item finder** now moves in `/flatui move`. Drag its box, or its title bar, and it stays where you let it go, a place your profile keeps as it keeps every moved frame; **Dock**, a right-click on its title or a right-click on its box hangs it on the window again, and **Undo** and **Reset all** know both. Its box shows only while the finder is open, so a closed finder is no longer in the way.
- **Hit table**, **On enemy tooltips**: the lines are shorter, a letter for each part and short labels, as in "Swing  M8 D6.5 P14 B5 G40 C12.1 H33.4" (M miss, D dodge, P parry, G glance, B block, C crit, H hit, in percent; a figure loses its .0).

## 0.5.20

- For WoW: Forever 1.60.1 build 70938. The interface number stays 16001.
- **Minimap and buffs**, **Tracker, loot and alerts**, new row **Questie's tracker** (on): with the Questie addon loaded, its quest tracker takes the look of the objective tracker. **Tracker background** puts Flat UI's background and thin border round it in place of Questie's own, **Tracker headers** a flat band with a thin border behind its **Questie Tracker** heading and each zone heading, **Heading size** sizes the zone headings, **Tracker item buttons** gives its quest item buttons a thin border and a cropped icon (**Icon crop**), and **Opacity in combat** fades it in a fight. While the **Fonts** page is on, its heading and lines take that page's **Interface font**, **Text outline** and **Text shadow**. Questie's own font sizes stay, as Questie lays its lines out by them, and it draws no bars, so **Tracker bars** has nothing to do there. Questie keeps its own look with the row off, and its settings are never changed. A Questie whose tracker Flat UI does not know is left alone, and `/flatui status` says so.
- **Chat and tooltips**, **Spells and gear**, new row **Total of a cast** (on): a spell's tooltip with power lines also says what one cast does now with your stats, base and bonus together, as in "Total 973 a cast (556 + 417)": a spell over time over its whole duration, with a tick, a channel its whole channel, a heal its healing, and a weapon strike one hit with the weapon you wear. New row **Average and rates** (on): a dim line under it with what a cast does on average with crits and misses (a heal with crits) against the enemy **Against** names, for a second of cast time (the global cooldown for an instant) and for a point of mana; none for rage and energy.
- **Spells and gear**: the spell and item lines have one look. A block of more than one line opens with a gold header; each figure has its label on the left in light grey and its value on the right in white; a change is green when it is better and red when it is worse, with its sign; ~ comes before an estimate. Numbers have thousands separated by commas, whole from 100 up and one decimal below, and percents one decimal (the avoidance table two). New row **Notes under the lines** (**Compact**): at most one dim line of notes under each block, its notes joined; **Full** shows every note.
- **Spells and gear**, new row **Overall change** (**Weighted**): an item's lines and the **Gear comparison** window open with one **Overall** line, as in "Overall Damage +2.4% · Threat +1.1% · Damage taken -2.1%", the change in damage, healing and threat over the spells you chose, each counted by its weight. Where avoidance shows, **Damage taken** says what the avoidance lines mean together: an enemy's swing after misses, dodges, parries, blocks, crits, crushing blows and your armor, with the item against without it. The block's header names what it compares against ("Instead of ..."). **Off** leaves the line out, and Damage taken then shows in the avoidance lines instead: it shows once either way.
- **Spells and gear**, new row **Enemy hit for block value** (1,500): the size of an enemy's hit before armor that your block value is measured against in **Damage taken**, an estimate.
- **Gear comparison** window: drag its bottom right corner to size it, from 380 by 220 to the size of the screen.
